# CoChem-TORQ SRS point-by-point review

This annex evaluates the supplied requirements; it does not assert that any described program feature has been implemented or scientifically validated. Line references are pinned to the retrieved baseline commit `d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e`, so later historical-document banners do not shift them. The canonical implementation SRS supersedes the duplicated historical drafts.

**Coverage:** 223 distinct numbered section headings (all repeated copies referenced), 25 explicit contracts, 345 checkbox proposals (165 Comprehensive and 180 Iterated), and eight AutoArchitect pillars. Comprehensive contains 145 substantive checklist entries and 20 placeholder entries; the latter do not supply missing research. All entries have dispositions. JSON inventory retains full original point text and source locations.

**Dispositions:** accepted = retain the idea with ordinary implementation validation; corrected = retain only the stated corrected requirement; deferred = research/optional capability requiring separate validation and, where a consequential choice is needed, a recorded decision; rejected = do not implement the stated unsupported rule. Accepted/corrected describes specification disposition, not completed production code. Citations in the original are unverified unless independently established by the canonical SRS evidence register.

## Chapter conclusions

- **[Chapter 1](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L5) — corrected.** Retain ecosystem boundary and typed contracts; remove unqualified sub-chemical/guaranteed accuracy claims, fabricated DRM, cryptographic misuse, ML and spectral pruning certainty. Explicit metadata, task scope and measurable acceptance are needed.
- **[Chapter 2](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L221) — corrected.** Replace monotonic ladder and conflicting numbering with capability registry; verify actual derivatives/properties and no-license revDSD. Post-HF methods can be less accurate for a given observable than a lower-numbered method.
- **[Chapter 3](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L408) — corrected.** Separate electronic point group, mass/isotopic symmetry and nonrigid PI group. Retain optional constrained symmetry with full-space stability diagnostics; no engine keyword guarantees saddle index. Remove proprietary GPU4SCF assertion pending actual package verification.
- **[Chapter 4](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L557) — corrected.** Define coordinate proposals, atom-matched endpoints, periodic grids, scoped reaction policy, budgets and TS/IRC validation. Safe defaults preserve all inputs and record selections. Geodesic/NEB methods are choices, not guaranteed global-path algorithms.
- **[Chapter 5](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L852) — corrected.** Define bounded adaptive exploration and matched inter-method relative-energy/observable comparisons. Distinguish numerical convergence, method stability and empirical accuracy. Record budget exhausted, maximum profile reached and unresolved coverage separately from success.
- **[Chapter 6](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L994) — corrected.** Minimum validation must reject one imaginary mode, not only more than one. Define optimizers, force/displacement units, external-mode projection and state continuity. DBOC is a diagonal Born-Oppenheimer correction, not a relativistic effect.
- **[Chapter 7](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L1182) — corrected.** Require cubic/semi-diagonal quartic force fields and resonance/applicability policy for VPT2. Keep Be/B0 distinct, property axes and full tensors. Ground-state rotational constants cannot be produced from geometry alone; arbitrary 30 versus 100 kcal/mol cutoffs conflict.
- **[Chapter 8](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L1390) — corrected.** Correct Watson A: Delta_J/Delta_JK/Delta_K/delta_J/delta_K; S: D_J/D_JK/D_K/d1/d2. Define actual Pickett files, IDs and units with round trips. HDF5 paths cannot simultaneously be dataset and group; Hessian-only VPT2 and automatic covariance claims are false.
- **[Chapter 9](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L1509) — corrected.** Validate actual JANPA/NBO capabilities and density conversion. Keep descriptive QTAIM/NCI/ELF interpretation distinct from energies, chemical graph identity and vibration-model validity; document grid errors, correlated density type and tool provenance.
- **[Chapter 10](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L1723) — corrected.** Keep CoChem-base UI integration and headless backend; define CLI/API equivalents and prior-approved batch policy. Latest EULA clarification supersedes fabricated tokens. Strong signatures are optional deployment threat-model choices, not a scientific prerequisite.
- **[Chapter 11](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L1869) — corrected.** Hardware routing follows engine/property/version capability and allocated resources, not tier or universal CPU/GPU assertions. Correct tblite/GPU assumptions, unverified GPU4SCF naming and SpycFit responsibility. Do not silently migrate unsupported electronic state/checkpoints.
- **[Chapter 12](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L2009) — corrected.** Define bounded, state-aware recovery categories and durable outcomes. No forced spin projection, active-space selection or DLPNO substitution without an approved profile. Diagnostics yield qualified warnings; preserve evidence and optional-property failure independently.
- **[Chapter 13](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L2241) — corrected.** Define isotope enumeration caps, actual isotope masses/spins/abundances, BO reuse and mass-dependent corrections. Minor isotopologues do not generally dominate all dense spectra. Placeholder twenty suggestions are absent.
- **[Chapter 14](https://github.com/ProfJJK-CoChem/CoChem-TORQ/blob/d7a4739a5f7d6f22ed659b32eeb4706bef16225e/docs/wiki/CoChem-TORQ_Comprehensive_SRS.md#L2248) — corrected.** Define a rapid single-conformer profile with supported engine, derivative route, numerical checks and labeled Be/B0 outputs; no promised 1–2% error. Keep full PES exploration bypass explicit and report absence of conformer coverage. Fifteen standard-processing placeholders contain no requirements.

## Decision policies and significant tradeoffs

Every point below links to one of these reviewed requirement policies. Its policy gives the explicit pros, cons and risks; its row supplies the point-specific correction. These shared policies deduplicate repeated claims without dropping their source identities.

<a id="policy-validate"></a>

### VALIDATE — corrected

Keep typed validated input, explicit units, isotope masses, charge/multiplicity/electron parity, provenance and atom maps. QCSchema and QCIO are separate versioned models; XYZ may enter through a validated converter with required metadata. Fingerprints are candidate filters, never a proof of graph isomorphism.

**Pros:** Detects malformed scientific input. **Cons:** Requires adapters and explicit missing-data states. **Risks:** Incorrect atom mapping or unrecorded topology inference can corrupt every derived property.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 4, 7.

<a id="policy-ml"></a>

### ML — deferred

ML engine/model changes require versioned model cards, element/charge/spin domains, independent validation, force/energy checks and out-of-domain handling. Keep ML optional; use reference calculations for publication claims.

**Pros:** Can reduce expensive evaluations. **Cons:** Training, calibration and model maintenance are substantial. **Risks:** Low ensemble disagreement can coexist with large systematic error; model transfer can remove real minima.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 5, 8, 11.

<a id="policy-uq"></a>

### UQ — corrected

Report calibrated prediction intervals only from matched held-out residuals or a documented uncertainty model with covariance. Otherwise report uncertainty unknown. ML variance, diagnostic thresholds, method-to-method differences and MAE are not confidence intervals.

**Pros:** Makes limits interpretable and testable. **Cons:** Needs domain-specific benchmark data. **Risks:** Fabricated or miscalibrated precision would mislead assignments and publication claims.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 11.

<a id="policy-budget"></a>

### BUDGET — corrected

Use an immutable versioned budget with authenticated actor, explicit limits and allowed recovery plans. SHA-256 checks integrity; authentication/signing is separate. Reserve costs atomically before dispatch; allow previously approved batch policies.

**Pros:** Bounds work and records decisions. **Cons:** Adds quota/state bookkeeping. **Risks:** Hash-only authorization is forgeable; GUI-only approval prevents unattended runs.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 6, 13.

<a id="policy-resource"></a>

### RESOURCE — corrected

Estimate actual per-engine memory/scratch/VRAM with cgroup and scheduler limits, empirical error margins, rank/thread counts and explicit units. Check measured usage; resource shortage stops or proposes an approved alternative without silently changing the model.

**Pros:** Prevents many avoidable allocation failures. **Cons:** Predictions need profiling and are not strict universal upper bounds. **Risks:** Polynomial formulas omit temporaries, auxiliary basis, rank replication and disk; forced method fallback changes science.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 5, 13.

<a id="policy-license"></a>

### LICENSE — corrected

Discover installed version, executable, real license/EULA conditions and administrator authorization. Remove invented ORCA/CFOUR authentication tokens and license-server assumptions; a local manifest records attestation, not legal proof.

**Pros:** Supports legitimate engines and portable installs. **Cons:** Site configuration differs. **Risks:** False DRM can block authorized users or falsely assert authorization.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 14.

<a id="policy-tier"></a>

### TIER — corrected

Represent named method/basis/dispersion/reference/derivative profiles in a capability registry. Tier numbers are organizational labels, not a monotonic accuracy ranking; use one canonical mapping across files.

**Pros:** Makes execution reproducible. **Cons:** Capability/version maintenance is needed. **Risks:** Changing tiers, reference states or basis silently invalidates comparisons.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 5.

<a id="policy-dh"></a>

### DH — deferred

Keep exact open-source revDSD as a gated extension until its SCF functional, spin-resolved MP2 components, coefficients, D3/D4 variants and response gradients match independently validated reference fixtures. Do not assemble ad hoc energy coefficients or promise analytic gradients.

**Pros:** Avoids dependence on one engine. **Cons:** Substantial theory, derivative and cross-engine validation work. **Risks:** Incorrect coefficient/dispersion/response combinations can produce smooth but wrong PESs.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 5, 15.

<a id="policy-sym"></a>

### SYM — corrected

Keep tolerance-aware point-group proposals, atom mapping and optional coordinate symmetrization with recorded displacement. Separate geometry constraints, electronic symmetry and spectroscopic symmetry. Validate full-space curvature; symmetry does not guarantee saddle order.

**Pros:** Reduces numerical ambiguity and supports expert control. **Cons:** Symmetric/linear/degenerate axes need special handling. **Risks:** Forced symmetry can create false stationary points; exact matching on noisy input can miss valid symmetry.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 7.

<a id="policy-pi"></a>

### PI — deferred

Recognize supported nonrigid models and retain PI metadata, but require feasible permutations, nuclear spin weights, validated torsion-rotation Hamiltonians and reference spectra before general automated PI assignment or tunneling prediction.

**Pros:** Necessary for nonrigid spectroscopy. **Cons:** Specialist model definitions and solvers are required. **Risks:** A torsional score or PI flag alone cannot derive correct groups, selection rules or splittings.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 7, 9.

<a id="policy-pes"></a>

### PES — corrected

Use periodic coordinates, explicit constraints, deterministic seeds, branch identity, relaxed forward/reverse or multi-start coverage and reference anchors. Count integer grid points with only proven symmetry reduction. Treat adaptive sampling as bounded exploration, never global-minimum proof.

**Pros:** Reduces redundant work and scan hysteresis. **Cons:** Sampling and branch-validation expense remains. **Risks:** Coarse/ML scans can miss narrow wells; dimensionality reduction can omit coupled motion.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 8.

<a id="policy-ts"></a>

### TS — corrected

Validate optimization tolerances, full projected mass-weighted Hessian and target saddle index: zero significant imaginary modes for minima, one for TS. Validate both IRC endpoints with atom mapping and internal-coordinate/topology checks; log numerical ambiguity.

**Pros:** Prevents wrong stationary-point assignments. **Cons:** Hessians/IRC add cost. **Risks:** A one-imaginary-frequency saddle need not connect intended endpoints; fixed RMSD alone is insufficient.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 7, 8.

<a id="policy-lam"></a>

### LAM — deferred

Add a versioned hindered-rotor/DVR/variational profile only with specified kinetic operator, mass metric, periodicity, coupling, basis/grid convergence, observables and applicability criteria. Preserve VPT2 validity diagnostics; do not switch using a universal barrier/dispersion threshold.

**Pros:** Can treat motions where perturbation theory is inadequate. **Cons:** New solvers and expensive PES/property surfaces. **Risks:** A 1D DVR is exact only for its chosen reduced Hamiltonian; omitted coupling can dominate error.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 9.

<a id="policy-vpt"></a>

### VPT — corrected

Separate harmonic ZPE, vibration-rotation constants and effective B0. VPT2 needs supported cubic/semi-diagonal quartic force fields, resonance treatment, mode conventions and convergence checks; a Hessian or frequency scaling alone does not supply general alpha constants.

**Pros:** Prevents mislabeled equilibrium or approximate values. **Cons:** Anharmonic derivatives are costly. **Risks:** Soft modes and resonances can invalidate perturbative treatment.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 9.

<a id="policy-spin"></a>

### SPIN — corrected

Report S2 with state/method and absolute-plus-relative policy appropriate to singlets/open shells. Flag diagnostics with context; do not infer exact failure or automatically replace references. Validate SCF stationarity/stability and preserve targeted excited/metastable state identity.

**Pros:** Detects suspect reference solutions. **Cons:** Expert review can remain necessary. **Risks:** Spin projection or RO switching changes the model; T1/D1 alone neither prove nor exclude multireference character.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 7, 10.

<a id="policy-mr"></a>

### MR — deferred

Require approved active orbitals/electrons, state roots and weights, root tracking, dynamic-correlation method and continuity tests. Automated/ML selection may propose active spaces but cannot silently resolve them.

**Pros:** Supports static correlation beyond single-reference methods. **Cons:** High computational cost and specialist validation. **Risks:** Inconsistent active spaces/root flips yield discontinuous surfaces; a lower variational energy is not proof of a better target state.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 10.

<a id="policy-bsse"></a>

### BSSE — corrected

Validate disjoint complete fragment atom maps, charges and multiplicities for CP requests. Keep raw, CP interaction, deformation and binding energies distinct; correct gradients when optimizing a CP PES. Apply correction only to compatible methods; record 3c/dispersion composition to avoid double counting.

**Pros:** Makes intermolecular energies and corrections traceable. **Cons:** Extra calculations and method-dependent choices. **Risks:** Universal forced CP/gCP or incompatible composite corrections can worsen results.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 8, 10.

<a id="policy-analytics"></a>

### ANALYTICS — corrected

Retain QTAIM/NCI/ELF and orbital descriptors with raw values, units, method/density provenance, numerical convergence and qualified interpretations. Do not treat BCP or Laplacian/energy-density thresholds as universal bond classification or VPT2 validity proof.

**Pros:** Produces useful reproducible chemical descriptors. **Cons:** Grid convergence and interpretation require care. **Risks:** Topology is not unique evidence of bond energy, dispersion mechanism or dynamics.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 10.

<a id="policy-nbo"></a>

### NBO — deferred

Verify each tool and version actually provides NPA/NAO/NBO/NRT/E2 operations and necessary Fock/density/orbitals; label JANPA-derived descriptors by their actual definition, without claiming identity with proprietary NBO. E2 terms are model-specific perturbative descriptors, not an additive binding-energy decomposition.

**Pros:** Adds interpretable orbital analysis. **Cons:** Adapters, licensing and reference validation differ. **Risks:** Unsupported capabilities or interchangeable branding can fabricate scientific results.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 10.

<a id="policy-wfn"></a>

### WFN — corrected

Preserve native wavefunctions plus validated converted artifacts with basis coefficients/order/normalization, density type, spin blocks, electron-count trace and orbital overlap checks. HDF5 is a project container, not automatically QCSchema/QCIO or lossless universal Molden.

**Pros:** Prevents silent cross-engine density corruption. **Cons:** Formats have capability/precision limitations. **Risks:** Natural/correlated/spinor/ECP wavefunctions are not always representable in a target format.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 4, 10.

<a id="policy-export"></a>

### EXPORT — corrected

Use versioned typed output schemas, units/axes/state labels, quality/source tags and authentic parser/round-trip fixtures. Keep observed .lin measurements distinct from predictions; validate Pickett IDs, signs and reductions against its actual manual. Preserve missing/unsupported separately from zero.

**Pros:** Makes artifacts interoperable and reviewable. **Cons:** Consumer contracts and format variants require tests. **Risks:** Incorrect parameter IDs, state labels or precision corrupt spectra despite valid text.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 12.

<a id="policy-props"></a>

### PROPS — corrected

Export requested available dipoles/EFG/quadrupole/spin-rotation/hyperfine properties with full axes, isotope constants, theory levels and convergence. EFG-to-chi uses nuclear quadrupole moment and units. Mandatory properties depend on target nuclei/state and solver capability.

**Pros:** Supports informative spectrum predictions. **Cons:** Different properties may need different methods. **Risks:** Dipoles alone do not predict instrumental SNR; unqualified exactness or forced tensor completeness is false.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 9, 12.

<a id="policy-isotope"></a>

### ISOTOPE — corrected

Reuse the Born-Oppenheimer electronic PES for chosen isotope substitutions, rediagonalize inertia and mass-weighted Hessians, rotate all tensors consistently and record abundance/degeneracy. Vibrational corrections, DBOC and populations are isotope-dependent.

**Pros:** Efficient isotope screening. **Cons:** Combinatorial enumeration must be bounded. **Risks:** Mass-only Be is not B0; substitution axes can rotate or become degenerate.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 7.

<a id="policy-kinetics"></a>

### KINETICS — deferred

Keep barriers and graph connectivity; add kinetics only in an explicit validated TST/RRKM/tunneling/master-equation profile with temperature, pressure, collisional and timescale assumptions. Do not prune conformers using one universal jet-cooling barrier.

**Pros:** Can assess trapping and relaxation. **Cons:** Experiment-specific collision/expansion modeling is required. **Risks:** Equilibrium Boltzmann weights or 1D transmission factors do not establish jet populations or line broadening.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 8, 9.

<a id="policy-ui"></a>

### UI — accepted

Add a CoChem-base view of persisted scientific state with visible assumptions, provenance and keyboard/headless equivalents. Manual edits create a new revision; UI refresh/disconnect cannot mutate or terminate work.

**Pros:** Makes computed results and operator choices inspectable. **Cons:** Frontend maintenance remains. **Risks:** UI-only checks cannot enforce backend constraints; display labels must not overclaim validation.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 14.

<a id="policy-orch"></a>

### ORCH — corrected

Use durable idempotent task state, restartable adapters, bounded retries, leases, cancellation and preserved raw logs/checkpoints; route by declared capability. Existing approval covers bounded batch work. Add framework adapters without mandating a wholesale rewrite.

**Pros:** Supports reliable unattended execution. **Cons:** Distributed operations and deployment need validation. **Risks:** Unbounded retries, duplicate jobs, unsafe cleanup or NFS SQLite WAL can lose work or exceed budgets.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 13.

<a id="policy-deploy"></a>

### DEPLOY — corrected

Use pinned reproducible environments and supported local/Slurm/container profiles with license-compliant external binaries. Probe actual CUDA/ROCm/MPI capabilities and keep CPU fallback where scientifically equivalent. Verify distributions and record hardware/software.

**Pros:** Improves deployment reproducibility. **Cons:** Containers and GPU/MPI portability still require site tests. **Risks:** Packaging cannot guarantee drivers, licenses, ABI or target capabilities.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 14.

<a id="policy-test"></a>

### TEST — corrected

Separate deterministic unit/contract tests, reference scientific fixtures and real-engine integration/benchmark suites. Report unavailable optional engines explicitly; release requires declared mandatory suites. Use tolerances justified by reference uncertainty and method/domain.

**Pros:** Makes release claims auditable. **Cons:** Licensed/HPC suites need suitable runners. **Risks:** Banning all test doubles/skips or universal experimental tolerances creates false failures or false coverage.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 15.

<a id="policy-research"></a>

### RESEARCH — deferred

Keep as a research extension pending primary-source verification, implementation availability, domain benchmarks and isolated acceptance criteria; do not make an unverified citation or performance claim normative.

**Pros:** Potential future accuracy or efficiency gain. **Cons:** Validation effort and maintenance are unresolved. **Risks:** Novel results may not generalize and should not replace converged reference calculations.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 15, 16.

<a id="policy-reject"></a>

### REJECT — rejected

Remove the stated rule because its claimed implication is scientifically or architecturally invalid; preserve the underlying diagnostic or data where useful without executing the unsupported automatic action.

**Pros:** Avoids biased or fabricated results. **Cons:** Some proposed automation is deliberately unavailable. **Risks:** Implementing the original rule would silently change scientific conclusions.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 2, 15.

<a id="policy-placeholder"></a>

### PLACEHOLDER — rejected

No actionable scientific requirement is supplied. Record the placeholder as reviewed; do not invent the missing twenty suggestions or treat a conversational audit claim as evidence.

**Pros:** Prevents false completeness. **Cons:** Original omitted ideas remain unavailable. **Risks:** Claiming these were implemented would misrepresent the source.
**Canonical SRS target:** sections 1.

## Material gaps required before claiming readiness

The original documents do not provide a complete executable contract for task states, derivative capabilities, numeric tolerances, units and tensor frames, method recipes, uncertainty calibration, data schema migration, concurrency, runtime installation, recovery semantics, authentication, benchmark cases or release evidence. The canonical SRS must define these explicitly. Research ideas marked deferred remain disabled unless their profile-specific implementation and independent validation pass. A specification can be ready for scoped implementation while deployment readiness remains contingent on actual end-to-end tests and scientific benchmark evidence.

Conversational statements about an Agent Council, a prior audit pass, SWEBOK/PMBOK compliance or flawless physics are not verification artifacts. They are removed from normative requirements.
